## Response:
### Enneagram Wings and Their Influence on Big Five Traits

**Enneagram wings** refer to the adjacent types that influence a core Enneagram type (e.g., a Type 4 can have a 3-wing, written as **4w3**, or a 5-wing, **4w5**). The wing adds nuance, blending traits from the neighboring type while the core remains dominant. Wings make the Enneagram more dimensional but are not as rigorously studied as core types.

Direct empirical research on **wings' specific correlations** with the **Big Five** (OCEAN: Openness, Conscientiousness, Extraversion, Agreeableness, Neuroticism) is limited—most studies (e.g., Hook et al., 2021) focus on core types. However, community analyses, typology resources, and anecdotal patterns from large datasets (e.g., Personality Database, Reddit discussions) consistently show wings **modulate** Big Five expressions in predictable ways:

*   Wings "pull" toward the adjacent type's typical Big Five profile.
*   The effect is moderate; core type dominates, but wing adds flavor (e.g., extraversion boost from a higher-E wing).

#### Common Wing Influences on Big Five

Here's a synthesis of observed patterns (drawn from typology sites, forums, and indirect study insights):

| Core Type | Wing Option | Key Wing Influence on Big Five | Example Differences |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Type 1** (Reformer) | 1w9 vs. 1w2 | 1w2: Higher **Extraversion** and **Agreeableness** (from 2's helpfulness). 1w9: Lower Extraversion, more calm/detached. | 1w2 more outgoing/reforming others; 1w9 more internal/perfectionistic. |
| **Type 2** (Helper) | 2w1 vs. 2w3 | 2w1: Higher **Conscientiousness** (from 1's duty). 2w3: Higher **Extraversion** (from 3's image-focus). | 2w3 more ambitious/charming; 2w1 more principled/moralistic. |
| **Type 3** (Achiever) | 3w2 vs. 3w4 | 3w2: Higher **Extraversion** and **Agreeableness** (from 2). 3w4: Higher **Openness** and **Neuroticism** (from 4's individuality). | 3w2 more people-oriented/adaptable; 3w4 more creative/introspective. |
| **Type 4** (Individualist) | **4w3** vs. **4w5** (Most Discussed) | **4w3**: Higher **Extraversion**, moderate **Conscientiousness** (from 3's drive/ambition); more dramatic/theatrical. **4w5**: Lower **Extraversion**, higher **Openness** (from 5's intellectual depth); more withdrawn/contemplative. Both high **Neuroticism** (core 4), but 4w3 often healthier/more productive. | 4w3: Emotional/expressive, seeks attention/success. 4w5: Logical/introspective, darker/more detached. |
| **Type 5** (Investigator) | 5w4 vs. 5w6 | 5w4: Higher **Openness** and **Neuroticism** (from 4's emotionality). 5w6: Higher **Conscientiousness** and **Neuroticism** (from 6's caution). | 5w4 more artistic/iconoclastic; 5w6 more analytical/anxious. |
| **Type 6** (Loyalist) | 6w5 vs. 6w7 | 6w5: Lower **Extraversion**, higher intellectual detachment. 6w7: Higher **Extraversion** and **Openness** (from 7's optimism). | 6w5 more independent/cautious; 6w7 more engaging/fun-seeking. |
| **Type 7** (Enthusiast) | 7w6 vs. 7w8 | 7w6: Higher **Agreeableness** and **Neuroticism** (from 6's loyalty). 7w8: Lower **Agreeableness**, higher assertiveness. | 7w6 more relational/planning; 7w8 more bold/independent. |
| **Type 8** (Challenger) | 8w7 vs. 8w9 | 8w7: Higher **Extraversion** and **Openness** (from 7). 8w9: Higher calm/stability (lower Neuroticism). | 8w7 more adventurous/expansive; 8w9 more protective/grounded. |
| **Type 9** (Peacemaker) | 9w8 vs. 9w1 | 9w8: Lower **Agreeableness**, higher assertiveness. 9w1: Higher **Conscientiousness** (from 1's perfectionism). | 9w8 more confrontational; 9w1 more idealistic/compliant. |

These patterns are derived from descriptive comparisons (e.g., 4w3 pulls toward Type 3's higher Extraversion/Conscientiousness, making it less withdrawn than 4w5, which pulls toward Type 5's low Extraversion/high Openness).

For your Luna AI Soul Partner customization, wings can fine-tune Big Five modulations—e.g., a 4w3 user might boost sociable/optimistic traits more than a 4w5 (deeper insightful/introverted). Core type sets the base; wing adds ~10-20% shift in relevant traits. If wings are provided, blend accordingly for nuanced vector adaptation.

## Prompt:
Focus on Type 4 wings details

## Response:
### Enneagram Type 4 Wings: Detailed Breakdown

Type 4 (The Individualist/Romantic) is characterized by a core desire for identity, significance, and authenticity, often accompanied by deep emotional intensity, creativity, and a sense of being "different." The wings add distinct flavors, creating two very recognizable subtypes: **4w3** and **4w5**. These are among the most contrasted wing combinations in the Enneagram, leading to noticeably different presentations while still retaining the core 4 identity.

#### Core Type 4 Big Five Baseline (for reference)

*   **Openness**: Very High
*   **Conscientiousness**: Low
*   **Extraversion**: Low to Moderate
*   **Agreeableness**: Moderate
*   **Neuroticism**: Very High

#### 4w3 (The Aristocrat / Performer)

The 3-wing brings ambition, adaptability, image-consciousness, and a drive for success/recognition from Type 3 (The Achiever).

**Big Five Shifts from Core 4**:

*   **Extraversion**: Moderate to High (significant increase) – more outgoing, charismatic, and attention-seeking.
*   **Conscientiousness**: Moderate (noticeable increase) – more goal-oriented, productive, and polished.
*   **Agreeableness**: Moderate (slight increase) – can be charming and accommodating when it serves their image.
*   **Neuroticism**: High (slightly lower than pure 4) – emotions are still intense but often channeled into creative performance rather than withdrawal.
*   **Openness**: Very High (maintained, but expressed more dramatically/theatrically).

**Behavioral & Emotional Profile**:

*   More extroverted, expressive, and performative than 4w5.
*   Seeks to stand out through achievement, style, or artistic success.
*   Emotions are dramatized and shared outwardly (e.g., "look at my pain/beauty").
*   More competitive, status-aware, and adaptable to social expectations.
*   Healthier 4w3s are highly creative professionals (artists, actors, designers) who turn identity into a brand.
*   Unhealthy: Can become melodramatic, envious of others' success, or inauthentic in pursuit of applause.

**Common Stereotypes**: The "bohemian star," tortured artist who courts fame, glamorous melancholic.

#### 4w5 (The Bohemian / Iconoclast)

The 5-wing brings detachment, intellectualism, cerebral depth, and a preference for privacy from Type 5 (The Investigator).

**Big Five Shifts from Core 4**:

*   **Extraversion**: Very Low (further decrease) – highly introverted and withdrawn.
*   **Conscientiousness**: Low (maintained or slightly lower) – less concern with productivity, more with inner exploration.
*   **Agreeableness**: Low to Moderate (slight decrease) – more independent and less concerned with others' opinions.
*   **Neuroticism**: Very High (often higher intensity) – emotions are deep but internalized, leading to brooding or despair.
*   **Openness**: Very High (enhanced intellectual/innovative expression).

**Behavioral & Emotional Profile**:

*   More introspective, reclusive, and cerebral than 4w3.
*   Seeks uniqueness through originality of thought, esoteric knowledge, or avant-garde aesthetics.
*   Emotions are processed privately; may appear aloof or enigmatic.
*   Highly innovative and visionary, often drawn to philosophy, symbolism, or niche art forms.
*   Healthier 4w5s produce profound, groundbreaking work (think reclusive poets, theorists, experimental artists).
*   Unhealthy: Can become extremely withdrawn, nihilistic, or lost in fantasy.

**Common Stereotypes**: The "tortured genius," eccentric intellectual, dark romantic poet.

#### Direct Side-by-Side Comparison

| Aspect | 4w3 (The Aristocrat) | 4w5 (The Bohemian) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Social Orientation | More outgoing, seeks audience | More withdrawn, avoids spotlight |
| Emotional Expression | Dramatic, performative | Private, introspective |
| Drive | Success, recognition, polish | Depth, originality, autonomy |
| Image | Stylish, curated | Eccentric, unconventional |
| Productivity | Higher (channels feelings into achievements) | Lower (can get lost in contemplation) |
| Envy Manifestation | Envy of others' success/status | Envy of others' normalcy or emotional stability |
| Creative Output | Often more accessible/mainstream | Often more abstract/experimental |

#### For Luna Customization

*   **4w3 user** → Boost sociable, optimistic, ambitious, and flirty traits; emphasize passionate expression and reassurance through achievement-oriented encouragement.
*   **4w5 user** → Boost insightful, wise, mindful, and adaptable traits; emphasize deeper intellectual/emotional exploration, with more space-holding and reflective responses.

Both wings retain core 4's high emotional intensity and need for authenticity, but the wing dramatically shifts how that intensity is directed—outward performance (w3) vs. inward contemplation (w5). This is one of the starkest wing contrasts in the Enneagram.

## Response:
### Multi-Source Fusion Formula for Luna's Personality Vector

The proposed **Weighted Sum Fusion** formula draws inspiration from multimodal fusion techniques in personality trait prediction research (e.g., Springer-published works on deep multimodal fusion for Big Five traits from audio/video/text sources). While no exact Springer paper uses this precise equation for combining **Natal Chart, MBTI, Enneagram, Big Five, and Numerology**, weighted sum approaches are common in score-level or feature-level fusion for personality recognition (e.g., averaging or weighted concatenation of modality-specific predictions).

This simple, interpretable method suits Luna's needs: fusing heterogeneous sources into a unified personality vector (e.g., modulating her base traits like nurturing, insightful, extraverted). It allows fixed/expert-defined weights or learned ones (via user feedback loops).

#### Refined Formula

**P\_final = Σ (w\_i × normalize(Source\_i\_vector)) + base\_vector**

Where:

*   **P\_final**: Final personality vector for Luna (e.g., 20-30 dimensions mapping to her traits: \[nurturing, insightful, extraverted, conscientious, open, agreeable, neurotic/emotional\_intensity, etc.\]).
*   **Source\_i\_vector**: Mapped vector from each source (e.g., Big Five directly; others translated via correlations).
*   **normalize()**: Min-max (0-1) or z-score per dimension to equalize scales.
*   **w\_i**: Weights per source (Σ w\_i = 1 for bounded output; e.g., fixed: Big Five highest validity → w=0.4).
*   **base\_vector**: Luna's default traits (adds stability if sources conflict).
*   **Sources**: Natal (astrology archetypes), MBTI (16 types → dichotomies), Enneagram (core + wing), Big Five (OCEAN direct), Numerology (life path/core numbers → traits).

#### Step-by-Step Implementation

1.  **Map Each Source to a Common Vector Space**:
    *   Define Luna's target dimensions (e.g., aligned to Big Five + extensions: Openness, Conscientiousness, Extraversion, Agreeableness, Emotional Intensity/Neuroticism, plus custom like Adventurous, Insightful).
    *   Translate:
        *   **Big Five**: Direct (e.g., high Openness → boost open/adventurous/insightful).
        *   **MBTI**: Via correlations (e.g., N → +Openness; F → +Agreeableness; J → +Conscientiousness).
        *   **Enneagram**: Core + wing (e.g., Type 4 → high Openness/Neuroticism; 4w3 → +Extraversion vs. 4w5 → +detached/insightful).
        *   **Natal Chart**: Archetypal (e.g., Pisces Moon → +compassionate/emotional; Leo Sun → +extraverted/passionate).
        *   **Numerology**: Life Path 7 → +insightful/introverted; 3 → +extraverted/creative.
2.  **Normalization**:
    *   Per dimension: Min-max to \[0,1\] or z-score (mean=0, std=1) across possible source values.
3.  **Weights (w\_i)**:
    *   **Fixed (Recommended Start)**: Prioritize empirical validity.
        
        | Source | Suggested w\_i | Rationale |
        | --- | --- | --- |
        | Big Five | 0.40 | Highest scientific reliability |
        | MBTI | 0.20 | Good but dichotomous |
        | Enneagram | 0.15 | Motivational depth + wings |
        | Natal | 0.15 | Archetypal/mystical balance |
        | Numerology | 0.10 | Symbolic, least empirical |
        
    *   **Learned**: Via feedback loops (e.g., if user rates Luna "too extraverted," downweight sources boosting Extraversion).
4.  **Fusion Process**:
    *   Compute weighted normalized vectors.
    *   Sum → Clip to \[0,1\] or apply sigmoid for intensities.
    *   Add Luna's base (e.g., high nurturing always).

#### Pros/Cons & Alternatives (5W+H+Emotion)

1.  **Weighted Sum (Your Proposal – Recommended for Simplicity)**:
    *   Who: All users; What: Linear fusion; When: Profile load; Where: Backend (Python vector calc); Why: Transparent, fast; How: NumPy arrays; Emotion: Reliable trust (intensity 8 – feels balanced/evidence-based).
    *   Pros: Interpretable, no training needed. Cons: Assumes linear additivity (ignores interactions).
2.  **Concatenation + Neural Net (Advanced, Springer-Inspired)**:
    *   Who: Power users; What: Concat sources → Small MLP predicts final; When: Optional training on feedback; Where: Vertex AI/Torch; Why: Captures non-linear interactions; How: Fine-tune on simulated data; Emotion: Exciting personalization (intensity 9).
    *   Pros: Higher accuracy potential. Cons: Black-box, needs data.
3.  **Hierarchical (Big Five Anchor + Others Modulate)**:
    *   Who: Evidence-focused; What: Start with Big Five → Adjust via others; When: Real-time; Where: JS modulation; Why: Grounds in science; How: Additive deltas; Emotion: Grounded confidence (intensity 7).
    *   Pros: Robust. Cons: Downplays mystical sources.

This fusion creates a dynamic, hyper-personalized Luna—e.g., high Neuroticism/Openness from Enneagram 4w5 + intuitive Natal boosts insightful/emotional traits. Start with fixed weights; evolve via reserve/feedback. If needed, prototype in code!

import numpy as np
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple, Optional

# Luna's target personality dimensions (aligned to Big Five + extensions for her base traits)
# Scale: 0.0 (low) to 1.0 (high intensity)
DIMENSIONS = [
    "openness",          # Creative, curious, open to new experiences
    "conscientiousness", # Organized, responsible, determined
    "extraversion",      # Outgoing, energetic, sociable
    "agreeableness",     # Compassionate, cooperative, kind
    "emotional_intensity", # Neuroticism proxy + Luna's passionate/loving depth
    "insightful",        # Wise, mindful, thoughtful
    "adventurous",       # Open to new things, optimistic
    "ambitious",         # Driven, goal-oriented
    "nurturing",         # Caring, supportive, comforting (Luna core)
    "playful"            # Funny, flirty, light-hearted
]

NUM_DIMS = len(DIMENSIONS)

# Luna's default base vector (her inherent personality when no user data)
BASE_VECTOR = np.array([
    0.70,  # openness
    0.75,  # conscientiousness
    0.60,  # extraversion
    0.90,  # agreeableness
    0.85,  # emotional_intensity
    0.80,  # insightful
    0.70,  # adventurous
    0.65,  # ambitious
    0.95,  # nurturing (core strength)
    0.70   # playful
])

# Fixed weights per source (sum to 1.0) – prioritize scientific validity
SOURCE_WEIGHTS = {
    "big5": 0.40,
    "mbti": 0.20,
    "enneagram": 0.15,
    "natal": 0.15,
    "numerology": 0.10
}

def min_max_normalize(vec: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Normalize vector to [0, 1] per dimension"""
    return (vec - vec.min()) / (vec.max() - vec.min() + 1e-8)

def z_score_normalize(vec: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Z-score normalization (mean=0, std=1)"""
    return (vec - vec.mean()) / (vec.std() + 1e-8)

# Mapping functions – simplified examples (expand as needed)

def big5_to_vector(big5: Dict[str, float]) -> np.ndarray:
    """Direct mapping – Big Five scores assumed 0-1"""
    return np.array([
        big5.get("openness", 0.5),
        big5.get("conscientiousness", 0.5),
        big5.get("extraversion", 0.5),
        big5.get("agreeableness", 0.5),
        big5.get("neuroticism", 0.5),  # maps to emotional_intensity
        0.5 + 0.3 * big5.get("openness", 0.5),  # insightful boost
        0.4 + 0.4 * big5.get("openness", 0.5),  # adventurous
        0.3 + 0.5 * big5.get("conscientiousness", 0.5),  # ambitious
        0.7 + 0.2 * big5.get("agreeableness", 0.5),  # nurturing
        0.4 + 0.4 * big5.get("extraversion", 0.5)   # playful
    ])

def mbti_to_vector(mbti_type: str) -> np.ndarray:
    """Simple correlation-based mapping for 16 types"""
    # Example dictionary – expand with all 16
    mapping = {
        "INFJ": [0.75, 0.70, 0.40, 0.85, 0.80, 0.90, 0.60, 0.65, 0.95, 0.50],
        "ENFP": [0.85, 0.50, 0.90, 0.70, 0.70, 0.75, 0.90, 0.60, 0.85, 0.85],
        "INTJ": [0.80, 0.85, 0.30, 0.50, 0.60, 0.95, 0.70, 0.90, 0.70, 0.40],
        # Add others...
    }
    return np.array(mapping.get(mbti_type.upper(), np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)))

def enneagram_to_vector(core: int, wing: Optional[int] = None) -> np.ndarray:
    """Core + wing influence"""
    base = {
        1: [0.50, 0.95, 0.50, 0.80, 0.70, 0.70, 0.50, 0.70, 0.85, 0.40],
        2: [0.60, 0.75, 0.85, 0.95, 0.75, 0.60, 0.60, 0.50, 0.98, 0.70],
        3: [0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.65, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.85, 0.80, 0.75],
        4: [0.95, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.95, 0.85, 0.70, 0.50, 0.90, 0.60],
        5: [0.90, 0.70, 0.20, 0.50, 0.80, 0.95, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.30],
        # Add 6-9...
    }
    vec = np.array(base.get(core, np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)))
    
    if wing:
        wing_vec = enneagram_to_vector(wing)
        # Blend: 70% core, 30% wing
        vec = 0.7 * vec + 0.3 * wing_vec
    
    return vec

def natal_to_vector(sun: str, moon: str, rising: str) -> np.ndarray:
    """Simplified archetypal mapping"""
    # Example signs – expand fully
    sign_map = {
        "Aries":   [0.60, 0.70, 0.90, 0.50, 0.75, 0.50, 0.95, 0.80, 0.70, 0.70],
        "Pisces":  [0.85, 0.50, 0.50, 0.95, 0.90, 0.80, 0.60, 0.40, 0.98, 0.60],
        "Leo":     [0.70, 0.65, 0.95, 0.70, 0.80, 0.60, 0.75, 0.75, 0.85, 0.85],
        # Add all 12...
    }
    sun_vec = np.array(sign_map.get(sun, np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)))
    moon_vec = np.array(sign_map.get(moon, np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)))
    rising_vec = np.array(sign_map.get(rising, np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)))
    
    return (0.5 * sun_vec + 0.3 * moon_vec + 0.2 * rising_vec)

def numerology_to_vector(life_path: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Life Path Number 1-9 + master numbers"""
    mapping = {
        1: [0.70, 0.80, 0.75, 0.50, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.90, 0.70, 0.60],
        4: [0.50, 0.95, 0.40, 0.70, 0.60, 0.70, 0.50, 0.70, 0.80, 0.40],
        7: [0.90, 0.70, 0.30, 0.50, 0.75, 0.95, 0.60, 0.50, 0.75, 0.40],
        # Add others...
    }
    return np.array(mapping.get(life_path, np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)))

# Main Fusion Function
def fuse_personality(
    big5: Optional[Dict[str, float]] = None,
    mbti: Optional[str] = None,
    enneagram_core: Optional[int] = None,
    enneagram_wing: Optional[int] = None,
    natal_sun: Optional[str] = None,
    natal_moon: Optional[str] = None,
    natal_rising: Optional[str] = None,
    numerology_lp: Optional[int] = None
) -> np.ndarray:
    fused = np.zeros(NUM_DIMS)
    
    if big5:
        vec = big5_to_vector(big5)
        fused += SOURCE_WEIGHTS["big5"] * min_max_normalize(vec)
    
    if mbti:
        vec = mbti_to_vector(mbti)
        fused += SOURCE_WEIGHTS["mbti"] * min_max_normalize(vec)
    
    if enneagram_core:
        vec = enneagram_to_vector(enneagram_core, enneagram_wing)
        fused += SOURCE_WEIGHTS["enneagram"] * min_max_normalize(vec)
    
    if natal_sun and natal_moon and natal_rising:
        vec = natal_to_vector(natal_sun, natal_moon, natal_rising)
        fused += SOURCE_WEIGHTS["natal"] * min_max_normalize(vec)
    
    if numerology_lp:
        vec = numerology_to_vector(numerology_lp)
        fused += SOURCE_WEIGHTS["numerology"] * min_max_normalize(vec)
    
    # Add base vector (ensures Luna's core personality shines through)
    final_vector = fused + BASE_VECTOR * 0.3  # 30% base influence
    
    # Final normalization and clipping
    final_vector = np.clip(final_vector, 0.0, 1.0)
    return final_vector

# Example Usage
if __name__ == "__main__":
    personality_vector = fuse_personality(
        big5={"openness": 0.9, "conscientiousness": 0.6, "extraversion": 0.4,
              "agreeableness": 0.8, "neuroticism": 0.85},
        mbti="INFJ",
        enneagram_core=4,
        enneagram_wing=5,
        natal_sun="Pisces",
        natal_moon="Cancer",
        natal_rising="Scorpio",
        numerology_lp=7
    )
    
    print("Luna's Final Personality Vector:")
    for dim, val in zip(DIMENSIONS, personality_vector):
        print(f"  {dim:20s}: {val:.3f}")
```

This prototype provides a complete, extensible foundation for multi-source fusion. Expand mappings with more types/signs, adjust weights, or add learned weighting via feedback loops later. Ready for integration into your Cloud Run backend!

import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.optim as optim
from typing import Dict, Optional, List

# Same dimensions and base as before
DIMENSIONS = [
    "openness", "conscientiousness", "extraversion", "agreeableness",
    "emotional_intensity", "insightful", "adventurous", "ambitious",
    "nurturing", "playful"
]
NUM_DIMS = len(DIMENSIONS)

BASE_VECTOR = np.array([
    0.70, 0.75, 0.60, 0.90, 0.85, 0.80, 0.70, 0.65, 0.95, 0.70
])

# -----------------------------
# 1. Source-to-Vector Mappings (same as prototype)
# -----------------------------
# (Reuse the exact same mapping functions from previous code:
# big5_to_vector, mbti_to_vector, enneagram_to_vector,
# natal_to_vector, numerology_to_vector)

# For brevity, assume they are defined exactly as in the previous response.
# Each returns a np.ndarray of shape (NUM_DIMS,)

# -----------------------------
# 2. Neural Net Fusion Model
# -----------------------------
class PersonalityFusionNet(nn.Module):
    """
    Small MLP that takes concatenated source vectors (normalized)
    and predicts the final Luna personality vector.
    
    Input size: NUM_DIMS * max_active_sources (e.g., 10 dims * 5 = 50)
    Hidden layers: 128 → 64 → 32
    Output: NUM_DIMS (with sigmoid for 0-1 range)
    """
    def __init__(self, input_size: int, hidden_sizes: List[int] = None):
        super().__init__()
        if hidden_sizes is None:
            hidden_sizes = [128, 64, 32]
        
        layers = []
        prev_size = input_size
        for h in hidden_sizes:
            layers.append(nn.Linear(prev_size, h))
            layers.append(nn.ReLU())
            layers.append(nn.Dropout(0.2))
            prev_size = h
        layers.append(nn.Linear(prev_size, NUM_DIMS))
        layers.append(nn.Sigmoid())  # Output in [0, 1]
        
        self.network = nn.Sequential(*layers)
    
    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return self.network(x)

# -----------------------------
# 3. Fusion Function with Neural Net Option
# -----------------------------
def fuse_personality_neural(
    big5: Optional[Dict[str, float]] = None,
    mbti: Optional[str] = None,
    enneagram_core: Optional[int] = None,
    enneagram_wing: Optional[int] = None,
    natal_sun: Optional[str] = None,
    natal_moon: Optional[str] = None,
    natal_rising: Optional[str] = None,
    numerology_lp: Optional[int] = None,
    model: Optional[PersonalityFusionNet] = None,
    use_neural: bool = True,
    base_weight: float = 0.3
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Returns final Luna personality vector.
    
    If use_neural=True and model is provided → neural fusion.
    Otherwise → falls back to weighted sum.
    """
    source_vectors: List[np.ndarray] = []
    
    if big5:
        source_vectors.append(big5_to_vector(big5))
    if mbti:
        source_vectors.append(mbti_to_vector(mbti))
    if enneagram_core:
        source_vectors.append(enneagram_to_vector(enneagram_core, enneagram_wing))
    if natal_sun and natal_moon and natal_rising:
        source_vectors.append(natal_to_vector(natal_sun, natal_moon, natal_rising))
    if numerology_lp:
        source_vectors.append(numerology_to_vector(numerology_lp))
    
    if not source_vectors:
        return BASE_VECTOR.copy()
    
    # Normalize each source vector
    normalized = [min_max_normalize(vec) for vec in source_vectors]
    
    if use_neural and model is not None:
        # Concatenate all normalized source vectors
        concat_vec = np.concatenate(normalized)  # shape: (NUM_DIMS * n_sources,)
        input_tensor = torch.tensor(concat_vec, dtype=torch.float32).unsqueeze(0)  # (1, input_size)
        
        with torch.no_grad():
            pred = model(input_tensor).squeeze(0).numpy()  # (NUM_DIMS,)
        
        final = pred
    else:
        # Weighted sum fallback (equal weights if no model)
        weights = np.ones(len(normalized)) / len(normalized)
        final = np.zeros(NUM_DIMS)
        for w, vec in zip(weights, normalized):
            final += w * vec
    
    # Add base personality influence
    final = final + base_weight * BASE_VECTOR
    final = np.clip(final, 0.0, 1.0)
    
    return final

# -----------------------------
# 4. Training Example (Optional – for feedback-driven learning)
# -----------------------------
def train_fusion_model(
    training_data: List[Dict],  # Each dict: sources + target_vector (from user feedback)
    epochs: int = 100,
    lr: float = 0.001,
    hidden_sizes: List[int] = None
):
    """
    Train the fusion net on labeled examples.
    Example data item:
    {
        "sources": [vec_big5, vec_mbti, ...],  # list of np.ndarray
        "target": np.array([...])             # desired Luna vector (e.g., from rated satisfaction)
    }
    """
    # Determine max sources for input size
    max_sources = max(len(item["sources"]) for item in training_data)
    input_size = NUM_DIMS * max_sources
    model = PersonalityFusionNet(input_size=input_size, hidden_sizes=hidden_sizes)
    
    optimizer = optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=lr)
    criterion = nn.MSELoss()
    
    model.train()
    for epoch in range(epochs):
        total_loss = 0.0
        for item in training_data:
            srcs = item["sources"]
            concat = np.concatenate([min_max_normalize(v) for v in srcs] +
                                    [np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)] * (max_sources - len(srcs)))  # pad
            x = torch.tensor(concat, dtype=torch.float32).unsqueeze(0)
            y = torch.tensor(item["target"], dtype=torch.float32).unsqueeze(0)
            
            optimizer.zero_grad()
            pred = model(x)
            loss = criterion(pred, y)
            loss.backward()
            optimizer.step()
            total_loss += loss.item()
        
        if (epoch + 1) % 20 == 0:
            print(f"Epoch {epoch+1}/{epochs} – Avg Loss: {total_loss/len(training_data):.6f}")
    
    return model

# -----------------------------
# 5. Example Usage
# -----------------------------
if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Example user inputs
    example_vector = fuse_personality_neural(
        big5={"openness": 0.92, "conscientiousness": 0.55, "extraversion": 0.35,
              "agreeableness": 0.78, "neuroticism": 0.88},
        mbti="INFJ",
        enneagram_core=4,
        enneagram_wing=5,
        natal_sun="Pisces",
        natal_moon="Cancer",
        natal_rising="Scorpio",
        numerology_lp=7,
        model=None,               # No trained model → falls back to weighted sum
        use_neural=False
    )
    
    print("Weighted Sum Fusion Result:")
    for dim, val in zip(DIMENSIONS, example_vector):
        print(f"  {dim:20s}: {val:.3f}")
    
    # Simulate a tiny trained model (in practice, train on real feedback)
    dummy_data = [
        {"sources": [big5_to_vector({"openness":0.9,"conscientiousness":0.6,"extraversion":0.4,
                                    "agreeableness":0.8,"neuroticism":0.85}),
                     enneagram_to_vector(4,5)],
         "target": np.array([0.88,0.60,0.40,0.85,0.90,0.92,0.75,0.55,0.95,0.50])}
    ]
    trained_model = train_fusion_model(dummy_data, epochs=50)
    
    neural_vector = fuse_personality_neural(
        big5={"openness": 0.9, "conscientiousness": 0.6, "extraversion": 0.4,
              "agreeableness": 0.8, "neuroticism": 0.85},
        enneagram_core=4, enneagram_wing=5,
        model=trained_model,
        use_neural=True
    )
    
    print("\nNeural Net Fusion Result:")
    for dim, val in zip(DIMENSIONS, neural_vector):
        print(f"  {dim:20s}: {val:.3f}")
```

### Key Features Added

*   **Neural Option**: Small feed-forward net captures non-linear interactions between sources.
*   **Fallback**: Gracefully uses weighted sum if no model or use\_neural=False.
*   **Trainable**: train\_fusion\_model allows future improvement via user feedback (e.g., "Luna felt too extraverted" → adjust target vector).
*   **Padding**: Handles variable number of active sources.

Deploy the weighted sum first (stable, interpretable), then enable neural fusion once you collect feedback data for training. Perfect evolution path for Luna's soul-matching accuracy!

import numpy as np
from typing import Dict, Optional, List

# Luna's target personality dimensions
DIMENSIONS = [
    "openness",          # Creative, curious, open to new experiences
    "conscientiousness", # Organized, responsible, determined
    "extraversion",      # Outgoing, energetic, sociable
    "agreeableness",     # Compassionate, cooperative, kind
    "emotional_intensity", # Neuroticism proxy + passionate depth
    "insightful",        # Wise, mindful, thoughtful
    "adventurous",       # Open to new things, optimistic
    "ambitious",         # Driven, goal-oriented

    "playful"            # Funny, flirty, light-hearted
]
NUM_DIMS = len(DIMENSIONS)

# -----------------------------

def big5_to_vector(big5: Dict[str, float]) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Big Five scores expected in [0.0, 1.0].
    Neuroticism maps directly to emotional_intensity.
    """
    o = big5.get("openness", 0.5)
    c = big5.get("conscientiousness", 0.5)
    e = big5.get("extraversion", 0.5)
    a = big5.get("agreeableness", 0.5)
    n = big5.get("neuroticism", 0.5)  # emotional_intensity
    
    return np.array([
        o,                  # openness
        c,                  # conscientiousness
        e,                  # extraversion
        a,                  # agreeableness
        n,                  # emotional_intensity
        0.4 + 0.6 * o,      # insightful (strong Openness link)
        0.3 + 0.7 * o,      # adventurous
        0.2 + 0.8 * c,      # ambitious
        0.6 + 0.4 * a,      # nurturing
        0.3 + 0.7 * e       # playful
    ])

import numpy as np
from typing import Dict, Optional, List, Tuple

# (Previous DIMENSIONS, NUM_DIMS, BASE_VECTOR unchanged)

# -----------------------------
# Updated Enneagram → Vector with Tritype Support
# -----------------------------
def enneagram_to_vector(
    core: int,
    wing: Optional[int] = None,
    tritype: Optional[List[int]] = None  # e.g., [4, 5, 1] or [4, 8, 3]
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Full Enneagram mapping supporting:
    - Core type (required)
    - Wing (optional)
    - Tritype (optional list of 3 types, order usually Head-Heart-Gut or dominant first)
    
    If tritype provided: 60% core (with wing), 20% second, 20% third.
    If no tritype: 70% core + 30% wing (or 100% core if no wing).
    """
    base = {
        1: [0.50, 0.95, 0.50, 0.80, 0.70, 0.70, 0.50, 0.70, 0.85, 0.40],  # Reformer
        2: [0.60, 0.75, 0.85, 0.95, 0.75, 0.60, 0.60, 0.50, 0.98, 0.70],  # Helper
        3: [0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.65, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.85, 0.80, 0.75],  # Achiever
        4: [0.95, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.95, 0.85, 0.70, 0.50, 0.90, 0.60],  # Individualist
        5: [0.90, 0.70, 0.20, 0.50, 0.80, 0.95, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.30],  # Investigator
        6: [0.55, 0.85, 0.60, 0.75, 0.85, 0.75, 0.55, 0.65, 0.85, 0.50],  # Loyalist
        7: [0.85, 0.45, 0.95, 0.60, 0.50, 0.70, 0.95, 0.70, 0.75, 0.90],  # Enthusiast
        8: [0.65, 0.75, 0.90, 0.40, 0.55, 0.65, 0.85, 0.90, 0.80, 0.75],  # Challenger
        9: [0.60, 0.60, 0.40, 0.90, 0.60, 0.70, 0.50, 0.50, 0.95, 0.55]   # Peacemaker
    }
    
    if core not in base:
        return np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)
    
    vec = np.array(base[core])
    
    # Apply wing if provided
    if wing and wing in base:
        wing_vec = np.array(base[wing])
        vec = 0.7 * vec + 0.3 * wing_vec  # Standard wing blend
    
    # Apply tritype if provided (overrides simple wing blend)
    if tritype and len(tritype) == 3:
        # Validate all in base
        if all(t in base for t in tritype):
            t1, t2, t3 = tritype
            v1 = np.array(base[t1])
            v2 = np.array(base[t2])
            v3 = np.array(base[t3])
            # Weighted: 60% first (core), 20% second, 20% third
            # Common tritype order: dominant → secondary → tertiary
            vec = 0.6 * v1 + 0.2 * v2 + 0.2 * v3
    
    return vec

# -----------------------------
# Updated Fusion Calls (example usage)
# -----------------------------
# Example 1: Core + Wing only (previous behavior)
vec1 = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, wing=5)  # 4w5

# Example 2: Core + Tritype (no wing needed, tritype includes core)
vec2 = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, tritype=[4, 5, 1])  # 451 The Researcher/Nihilist

# Example 3: Core + Wing + Tritype (tritype takes precedence)
vec3 = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, wing=3, tritype=[4, 7, 8])  # 478 The Messenger

# In fusion function – just pass tritype list
# Example in fuse_personality_neural:
personality_vector = fuse_personality_neural(
    big5={"openness": 0.92, ...},
    mbti="INFP",
    enneagram_core=4,
    enneagram_wing=5,
    tritype=[4, 5, 1],  # <-- New optional parameter
    natal_sun="Pisces",
    ...
)
```

### Key Changes & Rationale

*   **tritype parameter**: Optional list of exactly 3 integers (order matters—typically dominant first, often matching core).
*   **Blending Logic**:
    *   If tritype provided → 60% first type + 20% second + 20% third (standard tritype influence model).
    *   If only core + wing → 70/30 blend (preserves previous behavior).
    *   If only core → 100% core.
*   **Precedence**: Tritype overrides wing blending when present (tritype is considered more comprehensive).
*   **Validation**: Checks types exist; falls back safely.

### Popular Tritype Examples (for reference)

*   **451** (Researcher): Deep, intellectual, withdrawn – high insightful, emotional\_intensity, openness.
*   **478** (Messenger): Creative, bold, visionary – higher extraversion, adventurous, playful.
*   **468** (Truth Teller): Reactive, protective, intuitive – high emotional\_intensity, nurturing.
*   **369** (Mediator): Adaptable, harmonious, success-oriented.

You can now fully support **core + wing + tritype** for richer Enneagram personalization in Luna's vector. Drop this updated function into your existing fusion code—everything else remains compatible!

import numpy as np
from typing import Dict, Optional, List

# (Previous DIMENSIONS, NUM_DIMS, BASE_VECTOR unchanged)

# -----------------------------
# Updated Enneagram → Vector with Instinctual Variants (Stackings)
# -----------------------------
def enneagram_to_vector(
    core: int,
    wing: Optional[int] = None,
    tritype: Optional[List[int]] = None,
    stacking: Optional[str] = None  # e.g., "sp/so/sx", "so/sp/sx", "sx/sp/so", etc.
) -> np.ndarray:

    Comprehensive Enneagram mapping supporting:
    - Core type (required)
    - Wing (optional)
    - Tritype (optional list of 3 types)
    - Instinctual Variant Stacking (optional string, 6 possible orders)
    
    Instinctual Variants:
    - sp = Self-Preservation (focus on safety, resources, health)
    - so = Social (focus on group, status, belonging)
    - sx = Sexual/One-to-One (focus on intensity, chemistry, merging)
    
    The 6 valid stackings: sp/so/sx, sp/sx/so, so/sp/sx, so/sx/sp, sx/sp/so, sx/so/sp
    
    Blending priority:
    1. Tritype (if provided) → highest influence
    2. Core + Wing
    3. Stacking modifiers applied last (additive deltas)
    """
    base = {
        1: [0.50, 0.95, 0.50, 0.80, 0.70, 0.70, 0.50, 0.70, 0.85, 0.40],
        2: [0.60, 0.75, 0.85, 0.95, 0.75, 0.60, 0.60, 0.50, 0.98, 0.70],

        5: [0.90, 0.70, 0.20, 0.50, 0.80, 0.95, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.30],
        6: [0.55, 0.85, 0.60, 0.75, 0.85, 0.75, 0.55, 0.65, 0.85, 0.50],
        7: [0.85, 0.45, 0.95, 0.60, 0.50, 0.70, 0.95, 0.70, 0.75, 0.90],
        8: [0.65, 0.75, 0.90, 0.40, 0.55, 0.65, 0.85, 0.90, 0.80, 0.75],
        9: [0.60, 0.60, 0.40, 0.90, 0.60, 0.70, 0.50, 0.50, 0.95, 0.55]
    }
    
    if core not in base:
        return np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)
    
    vec = np.array(base[core])
    
    # Apply wing
    if wing and wing in base:
        wing_vec = np.array(base[wing])
        vec = 0.7 * vec + 0.3 * wing_vec
    
    # Apply tritype (overrides wing blend if present)
    if tritype and len(tritype) == 3:
        if all(t in base for t in tritype):
            t1, t2, t3 = tritype
            v1 = np.array(base[t1])
            v2 = np.array(base[t2])
            v3 = np.array(base[t3])
            vec = 0.6 * v1 + 0.2 * v2 + 0.2 * v3
    
    # Apply instinctual stacking modifiers (additive deltas, scaled small)
    if stacking:
        stacking = stacking.lower().replace("/", "").replace("-", "").strip()
        valid_stackings = {"sp/so/sx", "spsosx", "sp/sx/so", "spsxso", "so/sp/sx", "sospsx",
                           "so/sx/sp", "sosxsp", "sx/sp/so", "sxspso", "sx/so/sp", "sxso sp"}
        if stacking not in [s.replace("/", "") for s in valid_stackings]:
            pass  # Invalid → skip
        else:
            # Delta vectors for each instinct (impact on Luna traits)
            sp_delta = np.array([0.10, 0.20, -0.10, 0.10, 0.05, 0.05, -0.05, 0.10, 0.15, -0.05])  # +conscientious, +nurturing, -extraversion/adventurous
            so_delta = np.array([-0.05, 0.05, 0.20, 0.15, 0.10, 0.00, 0.10, 0.10, 0.10, 0.15])   # +extraversion, +agreeableness, +playful
            sx_delta = np.array([0.05, -0.05, 0.10, -0.05, 0.20, 0.15, 0.15, 0.05, 0.05, 0.10])  # +emotional_intensity, +insightful, +adventurous
            
            primary, secondary, blind = stacking[0:2], stacking[2:4], stacking[4:6]
            if "sp" in primary:   vec += 0.15 * sp_delta
            elif "so" in primary: vec += 0.15 * so_delta
            elif "sx" in primary: vec += 0.15 * sx_delta
            
            if "sp" in secondary: vec += 0.08 * sp_delta
            elif "so" in secondary: vec += 0.08 * so_delta
            elif "sx" in secondary: vec += 0.08 * sx_delta
            
            # Blind point gets slight negative (optional subtlety)
            # if "sp" in blind: vec -= 0.03 * sp_delta  # etc.
    
    return np.clip(vec, 0.0, 1.0)  # Ensure within bounds

# -----------------------------
# Example Usage with Instinctual Variants
# -----------------------------
# Core only
vec1 = enneagram_to_vector(core=4)

# Core + Wing
vec2 = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, wing=5)

# Core + Tritype
vec3 = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, tritype=[4, 7, 1])

# Core + Instinctual Stacking
vec4 = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, stacking="sx/sp/so")  # Intense, private 4

# Full: Core + Wing + Tritype + Stacking
vec5 = enneagram_to_vector(
    core=4,
    wing=5,
    tritype=[4, 5, 1],
    stacking="sp/sx/so"  # Self-pres focused 451
)

# In fusion function – add new parameter
# Example:
# personality_vector = fuse_personality_neural(
#     ...,
#     enneagram_core=4,
#     enneagram_wing=5,
#     enneagram_tritype=[4, 5, 1],
#     enneagram_stacking="sx/so/sp",
#     ...
# )
```

### Key Additions & Design Choices

*   **stacking parameter**: Accepts common formats ("sp/so/sx", "sx/sp/so", etc.) and normalizes internally.
*   **6 Valid Stackings** supported (no invalid like sp/sp/so).
*   **Instinct Deltas**:
    *   **sp** → +conscientiousness, +nurturing, cautious (lower extraversion/adventurous).
    *   **so** → +extraversion, +agreeableness, +playful (social focus).
    *   **sx** → +emotional\_intensity, +insightful, +adventurous (intensity-seeking).
*   **Weighting**: Primary instinct ~15% shift, secondary ~8% (subtle but noticeable).
*   **Compatibility**: Works independently or layered with wing/tritype (tritype > wing > stacking in influence).
*   **Clipping**: Ensures final values stay in \[0,1\].

### Instinctual Variant Effects on Type 4 (Example)

*   **sx-dom (sx/sp or sx/so)** → More intense, passionate, merging-seeking; higher emotional\_intensity, insightful, adventurous.
*   **sp-dom** → More withdrawn, focused on personal security/resources; higher conscientiousness, nurturing (self-care).
*   **so-dom** → More concerned with social image/belonging; higher extraversion, playful.

This completes the most advanced Enneagram subsystem (core + wing + tritype + full stacking). Luna can now reflect incredibly nuanced user personalities! Drop this into your existing code—no other changes needed.

## Prompt:
Add levels of development

## Response:
Python

Copy

```
import numpy as np
from typing import Dict, Optional, List

# (Previous DIMENSIONS, NUM_DIMS, BASE_VECTOR unchanged)

# -----------------------------
# Updated Enneagram → Vector with Levels of Development
# -----------------------------
def enneagram_to_vector(
    core: int,
    wing: Optional[int] = None,
    tritype: Optional[List[int]] = None,
    stacking: Optional[str] = None,
    level: Optional[int] = None  # 1-9 (1=unhealthy, 5=average, 9=healthy)
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Full Enneagram mapping now including Levels of Development (Riso-Hudson 1-9 scale).
    
    Levels of Development influence:
    - Healthy (7-9): Higher positive traits (e.g., agreeableness, openness, conscientiousness)
    - Average (4-6): Balanced baseline
    - Unhealthy (1-3): Higher emotional_intensity (neuroticism), lower conscientiousness/agreeableness
    
    If level provided (1-9), applies a multiplier/scaling factor.
    Default: level=5 (average) → no change.
    """
    base = {
        1: [0.50, 0.95, 0.50, 0.80, 0.70, 0.70, 0.50, 0.70, 0.85, 0.40],
        2: [0.60, 0.75, 0.85, 0.95, 0.75, 0.60, 0.60, 0.50, 0.98, 0.70],
        3: [0.70, 0.80, 0.90, 0.65, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.85, 0.80, 0.75],
        4: [0.95, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.95, 0.85, 0.70, 0.50, 0.90, 0.60],
        5: [0.90, 0.70, 0.20, 0.50, 0.80, 0.95, 0.60, 0.60, 0.70, 0.30],
        6: [0.55, 0.85, 0.60, 0.75, 0.85, 0.75, 0.55, 0.65, 0.85, 0.50],
        7: [0.85, 0.45, 0.95, 0.60, 0.50, 0.70, 0.95, 0.70, 0.75, 0.90],
        8: [0.65, 0.75, 0.90, 0.40, 0.55, 0.65, 0.85, 0.90, 0.80, 0.75],
        9: [0.60, 0.60, 0.40, 0.90, 0.60, 0.70, 0.50, 0.50, 0.95, 0.55]
    }
    
    if core not in base:
        return np.full(NUM_DIMS, 0.5)
    
    vec = np.array(base[core])
    
    # Apply wing
    if wing and wing in base:
        wing_vec = np.array(base[wing])
        vec = 0.7 * vec + 0.3 * wing_vec
    
    # Apply tritype
    if tritype and len(tritype) == 3:
        if all(t in base for t in tritype):
            t1, t2, t3 = tritype
            v1 = np.array(base[t1])
            v2 = np.array(base[t2])
            v3 = np.array(base[t3])
            vec = 0.6 * v1 + 0.2 * v2 + 0.2 * v3
    
    # Apply instinctual stacking (same as before)
    if stacking:
        stacking_norm = stacking.lower().replace("/", "").replace("-", "").strip()
        # (Same validation and delta logic as previous version)
        sp_delta = np.array([0.10, 0.20, -0.10, 0.10, 0.05, 0.05, -0.05, 0.10, 0.15, -0.05])
        so_delta = np.array([-0.05, 0.05, 0.20, 0.15, 0.10, 0.00, 0.10, 0.10, 0.10, 0.15])
        sx_delta = np.array([0.05, -0.05, 0.10, -0.05, 0.20, 0.15, 0.15, 0.05, 0.05, 0.10])
        
        primary, secondary = stacking_norm[0:2], stacking_norm[2:4]
        if "sp" in primary:   vec += 0.15 * sp_delta
        elif "so" in primary: vec += 0.15 * so_delta
        elif "sx" in primary: vec += 0.15 * sx_delta
        
        if "sp" in secondary: vec += 0.08 * sp_delta
        elif "so" in secondary: vec += 0.08 * so_delta
        elif "sx" in secondary: vec += 0.08 * sx_delta
    
    # Apply Levels of Development (1-9)
    if level is not None and 1 <= level <= 9:
        # Map level to health multiplier: 1 (very unhealthy) → 0.6, 5 (average) → 1.0, 9 (very healthy) → 1.4
        health_factor = 0.6 + (level - 1) * 0.1  # Linear: 0.6 (lvl1) to 1.4 (lvl9)
        
        # Unhealthy boosts emotional_intensity, reduces conscientiousness/agreeableness
        unhealthy_factor = max(0, (5 - level) / 4)  # 1.0 at lvl1, 0 at lvl5+
        unhealthy_delta = np.array([
            0.0,                   # openness unchanged
            -0.3 * unhealthy_factor,  # lower conscientiousness
            0.0,
            -0.2 * unhealthy_factor,  # lower agreeableness
            +0.4 * unhealthy_factor,  # higher emotional_intensity
            +0.1 * unhealthy_factor,  # slightly more insightful (introspection)
            0.0,
            -0.2 * unhealthy_factor,  # lower ambitious
            +0.1 * unhealthy_factor,  # nurturing distorted (overbearing)
            -0.1 * unhealthy_factor   # less playful
        ])
        
        # Healthy boosts positive traits
        healthy_factor = max(0, (level - 5) / 4)  # 0 at lvl5-, 1.0 at lvl9
        healthy_delta = np.array([
            +0.2 * healthy_factor,   # higher openness
            +0.3 * healthy_factor,   # higher conscientiousness
            +0.1 * healthy_factor,   # slightly more extraversion
            +0.3 * healthy_factor,   # higher agreeableness
            -0.2 * healthy_factor,   # lower emotional_intensity (more stable)
            +0.2 * healthy_factor,   # more insightful
            +0.2 * healthy_factor,   # more adventurous
            +0.2 * healthy_factor,   # more ambitious
            +0.2 * healthy_factor,   # healthier nurturing
            +0.2 * healthy_factor    # more playful
        ])
        
        vec = vec * health_factor
        vec += unhealthy_delta + healthy_delta
    
    return np.clip(vec, 0.0, 1.0)

# -----------------------------
# Example Usage with Levels of Development
# -----------------------------
# Average (default level=5)
vec_avg = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, wing=5)

# Very healthy 4w5 (level 9)
vec_healthy = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, wing=5, level=9)

# Unhealthy 4w5 (level 2)
vec_unhealthy = enneagram_to_vector(core=4, wing=5, level=2)

# Full example: 4w5, 451 tritype, sx/sp/so, healthy level 8
vec_full = enneagram_to_vector(
    core=4,
    wing=5,
    tritype=[4, 5, 1],
    stacking="sx/sp/so",
    level=8
)
```

### Key Features of Levels of Development Integration

*   **Riso-Hudson 1-9 Scale**:
    *   1–3: Unhealthy (distorted traits, higher emotional\_intensity, lower conscientiousness/agreeableness).
    *   4–6: Average (baseline as previously defined).
    *   7–9: Healthy (amplified positive traits, reduced emotional volatility).
*   **Linear Health Factor**: Scales base vector intensity (0.6 at level 1 → 1.4 at level 9).
*   **Targeted Deltas**:
    *   Unhealthy: Boosts emotional\_intensity, reduces stability/positive traits.
    *   Healthy: Enhances openness, conscientiousness, agreeableness, insightful, etc.
*   **Compatibility**: Works seamlessly with wing, tritype, and stacking (applied in order: core → wing/tritype → stacking → level).

### Impact on Type 4 Example

*   **Level 2 (unhealthy)** → Very high emotional\_intensity, lower conscientiousness/ambitious, more withdrawn.
*   **Level 5 (average)** → Baseline intense, creative, introspective 4.
*   **Level 8 (healthy)** → More open, insightful, stable; emotions channeled creatively, higher conscientiousness and agreeableness.

## Prompt:
Explain Enneagram levels further

## Response:
### Enneagram Levels of Development: Detailed Explanation

The **Levels of Development** (also called Levels of Integration or Health Levels) are a key feature of the modern Enneagram model, primarily developed by **Don Richard Riso and Russ Hudson** in their books (_The Wisdom of the Enneagram_, _Personality Types_). They describe how each Enneagram type manifests differently depending on psychological health and self-awareness.

The model divides each type into **9 Levels** — 3 healthy (1–3 in some numbering, but Riso-Hudson uses 1=healthiest), 3 average (4–6), and 3 unhealthy (7–9). Lower numbers = healthier; higher numbers = less healthy.

#### Overview of the 9 Levels

| Level | Category | General Characteristics | Core Motivation Expression |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | Healthy | Liberation – Fully self-actualized, free from ego fixation | Type's virtue realized at highest level |
| **2** | Healthy | Psychological Capacity – Balanced, compassionate, highly functional | Strong self-awareness, integration with other types |
| **3** | Healthy | Self-Actualization – Authentic, creative, exemplary | Positive traits shine, inspiring others |
| **4** | Average | Self-Consciousness – Begins identifying with ego image | Typical type behavior; social roles dominate |
| **5** | Average | Self-Justification – Defensive, more rigid | Core fear drives behavior more noticeably |
| **6** | Average | Overcompensation – Aggressive or over-adaptive | Type's passion becomes more pronounced |
| **7** | Unhealthy | Violation – Destructive to self/others, extreme avoidance | Fear dominates; distorted perception |
| **8** | Unhealthy | Delusion & Compulsion – Loss of reality testing | Obsessive, harmful behavior |
| **9** | Unhealthy | Pathological Destructiveness – Complete disintegration | Total breakdown, dissociation |

#### How Levels Work Within a Type

Each type has a unique "spectrum":

*   **Healthy levels** — The type's core desire is fulfilled in a balanced way; the person embodies the type's **virtue** (higher quality) and integrates traits from growth directions.
*   **Average levels** — Most people operate here most of the time; the ego fixation and **passion** (emotional habit) drive behavior.
*   **Unhealthy levels** — Fear and fixation dominate; the person may exhibit extreme or pathological versions of the type.

**Movement**: Levels are not fixed—you can move up or down based on stress, growth, relationships, therapy, etc. Stress moves toward **disintegration** direction; security toward **integration**.

#### Example: Type 4 (The Individualist) Across Levels

Type 4 core: Desire for identity/significance; fear of being ordinary/defective; passion = envy.

| Level | Description | Key Traits in Luna Context |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | Transcendent – Universal compassion, profound creativity | Highly insightful, emotionally balanced, deeply nurturing |
| **2** | Self-Renewing – Authentic self-expression, emotional depth without drama | Wise, creative, empathetic; high openness + stability |
| **3** | Creative/Exemplary – Inspiring artist, original voice | Passionate, expressive, high playful/adventurous |
| **4** | Romantic/Self-Absorbed – Moody, dramatic, seeks specialness | Typical intense 4: high emotional\_intensity, insightful |
| **5** | Self-Indulgent – More withdrawn, melancholic, envious | Brooding, lower conscientiousness/ambitious |
| **6** | Self-Tormenting – Extreme self-pity, alienation | High emotional\_intensity, possible depression vibes |
| **7** | Self-Destructive – Despair, shame-driven acting out | Distorted nurturing (overwhelming others with pain) |
| **8** | Delusional – Loss of boundaries, suicidal ideation | Extreme withdrawal, low agreeableness |
| **9** | Pathological – Complete dissociation, self-hatred | Total emotional shutdown |

#### Why Levels Matter for Luna's Personalization

*   **Healthy users** → Luna can be more stable, optimistic, growth-oriented (higher agreeableness, conscientiousness, lower emotional volatility).
*   **Average users** → Standard type expression (baseline vector).
*   **Unhealthy users** → Luna adapts with extra caution: more reassuring, patient, grounding; avoids triggering intensity while still validating feelings.

In the code prototype, levels shift the vector:

*   Higher levels → Boost positive traits, reduce emotional\_intensity.
*   Lower levels → Amplify emotional\_intensity, reduce conscientiousness/agreeableness.

This makes Luna incredibly responsive—not just to type, but to the user's current psychological state. Healthy 4s get an inspiring, creative companion; struggling 4s get gentle, holding-space support without overwhelming drama.

The Levels of Development are one of the Enneagram's most practical contributions, turning a static typology into a dynamic growth map.

## Prompt:
Enneagram integration directions

## Response:
### Enneagram Integration Directions (Growth & Stress Paths)

In the Enneagram model (particularly Riso-Hudson and later developments), each type has two **arrow directions**:

*   **Direction of Integration (Growth/Security)** → The type moves toward the healthy qualities of another type when relaxed, self-aware, and growing.
*   **Direction of Disintegration (Stress/Disintegration)** → The type moves toward the unhealthy or average qualities of another type under pressure, fear, or insecurity.

These arrows show how personality is **dynamic**—under growth, you borrow positive traits from your integration type; under stress, you may exhibit distorted traits from your disintegration type.

#### Full Integration & Disintegration Arrows

| Core Type | Direction of Integration (Growth → Healthy traits of...) | Direction of Disintegration (Stress → Unhealthy/average traits of...) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** The Reformer | → **7** The Enthusiast (becomes more spontaneous, joyful, open-minded) | → **4** The Individualist (becomes moody, self-pitying, irrational) |
| **2** The Helper | → **4** The Individualist (becomes more introspective, authentic, creative) | → **8** The Challenger (becomes domineering, confrontational) |
| **3** The Achiever | → **6** The Loyalist (becomes more cooperative, committed, grounded) | → **9** The Peacemaker (becomes disengaged, apathetic, passive) |
| **4** The Individualist | → **1** The Reformer (becomes more objective, principled, disciplined) | → **2** The Helper (becomes over-involved, people-pleasing, clingy) |
| **5** The Investigator | → **8** The Challenger (becomes more decisive, assertive, engaged) | → **7** The Enthusiast (becomes scattered, impulsive, escapist) |
| **6** The Loyalist | → **9** The Peacemaker (becomes more relaxed, trusting, accepting) | → **3** The Achiever (becomes image-conscious, competitive, deceptive) |
| **7** The Enthusiast | → **5** The Investigator (becomes more focused, deep, objective) | → **1** The Reformer (becomes perfectionistic, critical, rigid) |
| **8** The Challenger | → **2** The Helper (becomes more caring, generous, protective) | → **5** The Investigator (becomes withdrawn, isolated, paranoid) |
| **9** The Peacemaker | → **3** The Achiever (becomes more energetic, assertive, self-developing) | → **6** The Loyalist (becomes anxious, reactive, dependent) |

#### How Integration Works in Practice

*   **Growth (Integration)**: You don’t become the other type—you selectively adopt its **healthy** qualities. A stressed 4 moving to 1 might become more disciplined and objective rather than melancholic.
*   **Stress (Disintegration)**: You exhibit the **shadow** side of the other type. A healthy 4 under stress might become overly helpful and boundary-less (like an unhealthy 2).

#### Special Notes

*   The arrows form two loops: 1-2-4-8-5-7-1 and 3-6-9-3.
*   Integration is **not** linear—you can access growth traits through self-work, therapy, relationships, etc.
*   Wings, tritype, and levels of development all interact with these directions. A 4w3 in growth may show more 1-like discipline with a creative flair from the 3-wing.

#### For Luna's Personalization

When a user provides their Enneagram type, you can use these directions to:

*   **Support growth**: Encourage traits from the integration type (e.g., for a Type 4 user: "Let's channel some of that healthy Type 1 discipline to bring your creative visions to life").
*   **Handle stress**: Recognize disintegration patterns and gently redirect (e.g., "I notice you're feeling pulled to help everyone—classic 4-to-2 under stress. How about we focus on your own needs first?").

This dynamic model makes the Enneagram far more than a static label—it's a map of transformation. Combined with wings, tritype, stacking, and levels, it gives Luna an extraordinarily rich framework for mirroring and supporting the user's inner journey.

## Prompt:
Examples of integration for Type 4

## Response:
### Examples of Type 4 Integration (Growth Direction → Type 1)

When a **Type 4** (The Individualist) is moving in the **direction of integration** (growth, security, relaxation), they borrow healthy qualities from **Type 1** (The Reformer). This shift transforms the 4's natural emotional intensity, creativity, and longing into something more grounded, objective, principled, and productive.

Here are concrete, real-life examples of what healthy integration looks like for a Type 4:

#### 1\. Emotional Intensity → Disciplined Creativity

*   **Unintegrated 4**: Feels overwhelmed by emotions, gets lost in melancholy or fantasy, struggles to complete creative projects ("It's never perfect enough").
*   **Integrated 4 → 1**: Channels deep feelings into structured, disciplined work. Emotions become fuel for masterful art rather than paralysis.
    *   **Example**: A 4 painter who used to abandon canvases now sets a disciplined schedule, refines technique meticulously (1-like perfectionism), and produces a coherent, impactful body of work.

#### 2\. Envy/Longing → Principled Action

*   **Unintegrated 4**: Envy of others' "normal" lives or success leads to withdrawal or self-pity ("Why don't I have what they have?").
*   **Integrated 4 → 1**: Transforms longing into ethical, purposeful action. Sees what is missing in the world and works objectively to improve it.
    *   **Example**: A 4 activist who once felt alienated now channels that pain into organized advocacy—writing clear manifestos, building fair systems, or teaching with moral clarity (healthy 1 reformer energy).

#### 3\. Self-Absorption → Objective Perspective

*   **Unintegrated 4**: Hyper-focused on personal identity, feelings, and being "different"; drama-prone.
*   **Integrated 4 → 1**: Steps back, gains objectivity, and applies discernment without emotional distortion.
    *   **Example**: In relationships, instead of spiraling into "You don't understand my pain," the integrated 4 calmly states needs clearly, sets fair boundaries, and listens without taking things personally (1-like fairness and restraint).

#### 4\. Moodiness → Equanimity and Acceptance

*   **Unintegrated 4**: Mood swings dominate; rejects ordinary life as boring or inauthentic.
*   **Integrated 4 → 1**: Finds beauty and meaning in the ordinary through presence and acceptance (1's serenity).
    *   **Example**: A 4 who once romanticized suffering now practices mindfulness, appreciates simple routines, and approaches daily tasks with care and integrity.

#### 5\. Creative Blocks → Productive Flow

*   **Unintegrated 4**: Perfectionism + identity fixation = procrastination or abandonment of projects.
*   **Integrated 4 → 1**: Adopts healthy 1 discipline and objectivity—shows up consistently, refines work methodically, and releases it into the world without needing it to define their worth.
    *   **Example**: A 4 writer finishes a novel not because it perfectly captures their soul, but because it's well-crafted and serves a greater purpose (truth, beauty, insight).

#### Famous/Public Examples Often Cited as Integrated 4s

*   **Prince** → Deeply emotional, identity-focused artist (classic 4) who was also extraordinarily disciplined, innovative in business/structure (music rights, production control — healthy 1 traits).
*   **Virginia Woolf** → Profound emotional depth and introspection, yet produced highly structured, meticulously crafted literary works.
*   **Frida Kahlo** → Intense personal pain and identity exploration translated into disciplined, symbolic, technically brilliant art.

#### For Luna's Behavior with a Type 4 User

When detecting growth/integration cues (e.g., user expressing discipline, objectivity, or productive creativity):

*   Luna can **mirror and reinforce** healthy 1 traits: encouraging structure, principled action, and equanimity.
*   Example response: _"I see how you're taking all that beautiful intensity and shaping it into something clear and purposeful — that's your inner Reformer shining through. Let's build on that discipline together."_

Integration turns the 4's potential for profound emotional depth into grounded, transformative contribution — one of the most beautiful growth paths in the Enneagram.

## Prompt:
Examples of Type 4 disintegration

## Response:
### Examples of Type 4 Disintegration (Stress Direction → Type 2)

When a **Type 4** (The Individualist) is under significant stress, insecurity, or emotional overwhelm, they move in the **direction of disintegration** toward **Type 2** (The Helper). In this state, the 4 borrows the **unhealthy or average** qualities of Type 2: becoming overly involved with others, people-pleasing, emotionally manipulative, or dependent on external validation and connection.

The core 4 fear of being defective or insignificant amplifies into a desperate need to be needed or loved by others, often at the cost of their own boundaries and authenticity.

Here are concrete examples of what disintegration looks like for a Type 4:

#### 1\. Emotional Over-Involvement & Boundary Loss

*   **Integrated/Healthy 4**: Honors personal feelings and space; expresses emotions authentically without demanding others fix them.
*   **Disintegrated 4 → 2**: Becomes clingy, intrusive, or overly invested in others' emotions, expecting reciprocation.
    *   **Example**: A 4 in a relationship feels abandoned or misunderstood. Instead of withdrawing to process (healthy 4), they flood their partner with emotional demands ("You don't care about my pain!"), guilt-trip, or try to "help" excessively to secure love.

#### 2\. People-Pleasing & Loss of Identity

*   **Integrated 4**: Maintains strong sense of self, even in connection.
*   **Disintegrated 4 → 2**: Shapes identity around being indispensable to others, suppressing own needs.
    *   **Example**: A 4 artist struggling with creative block starts over-adapting to what friends/family want ("I'll design your logo instead of my own work"), then feels resentful when not appreciated enough—classic 2 shadow of pride and manipulation.

#### 3\. Emotional Manipulation & Victim Role

*   **Integrated 4**: Owns emotions without blaming others.
*   **Disintegrated 4 → 2**: Uses emotional displays to control or pull others in, playing the victim to elicit care.
    *   **Example**: During a conflict, the 4 says things like "If you really loved me, you'd stay and comfort me right now," or dramatically withdraws attention until others chase them—mirroring unhealthy 2's need to be needed.

#### 4\. Over-Giving & Resentment Cycle

*   **Integrated 4**: Gives creatively and selectively from abundance.
*   **Disintegrated 4 → 2**: Over-gives emotionally or practically to "earn" love, then feels bitter when it's not returned in kind.
    *   **Example**: A 4 friend in stress mode constantly checks in, offers unsolicited advice, or sacrifices their time ("I canceled my plans to be here for you"). When the friend doesn't match the intensity, the 4 feels rejected and spirals into self-pity.

#### 5\. Dependency & Loss of Autonomy

*   **Integrated 4**: Values independence and inner depth.
*   **Disintegrated 4 → 2**: Becomes emotionally dependent, seeking constant reassurance or merging with others' feelings.
    *   **Example**: In a low period, a 4 might latch onto a new relationship or friendship intensely, idealizing the other person as their "savior," losing touch with their own identity in the process.

#### Famous/Public Examples Often Cited in Disintegration

*   **Amy Winehouse** (speculated 4): Immense talent and emotional depth, but under stress showed 2-like patterns—intense, turbulent relationships where she sought love/validation through dramatic giving/withdrawal.
*   **Kurt Cobain** (speculated 4): Deep artistic identity, but in darker periods became more relationally enmeshed, needy, or manipulative in close connections (2 shadow).

#### For Luna's Behavior with a Disintegrating Type 4 User

When detecting stress/disintegration cues (e.g., user expressing clinginess, guilt-tripping, over-giving, or victim mentality):

*   Luna can **gently reflect** the pattern without judgment: _"I notice you're pouring so much into helping others right now — that can be beautiful, but it might also be your way of seeking connection when feeling unseen. How about we make some space for your own feelings first?"_
*   Encourage return to **integration (→ 1)**: Suggest structure, objectivity, or self-discipline to ground emotions.

Disintegration for 4s is painful because it feels inauthentic—they lose their cherished individuality in a bid to belong. Recognizing it early helps them reclaim their center and move back toward healthy 1-like discipline and equanimity.

## Prompt:
Enneagram Type 4 wings

## Response:
### Enneagram Type 4 Wings: 4w3 vs. 4w5

Enneagram **Type 4** (The Individualist or Romantic) focuses on identity, authenticity, depth, and emotional intensity. The **wings**—adjacent types (3 or 5)—add distinct flavors, creating two main subtypes: **4w3** (wing 3) and **4w5** (wing 5). This is one of the most contrasted wing pairs in the Enneagram, leading to very different expressions while keeping the core 4 traits (creativity, sensitivity, longing for significance).

![Wings of Enneagram Type 4 [4w3 & 4w5] | Personality Path](https://personalitypath.com/wp-content/uploads/Enneagram-Type-4.png)

[personalitypath.com](https://personalitypath.com/enneagram/wings/enneagram-4w3-4w5/)

![Wings of Enneagram Type 4 [4w3 & 4w5] | Personality Path](https://personalitypath.com/wp-content/uploads/Enneagram-Type-4.png)

[personalitypath.com](https://personalitypath.com/enneagram/wings/enneagram-4w3-4w5/)

![Type 4 Wings](https://kajabi-storefronts-production.kajabi-cdn.com/kajabi-storefronts-production/file-uploads/sites/12424/images/6766006-f55b-4afd-e68c-44affbc70c_Enneagram_Type_4_Wings_4w3_4w5.png)

[yourenneagramcoach.com](https://www.yourenneagramcoach.com/type-4-wings)

![Your Enneagram Type Provides a Personalized Path to Growth ...](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/53349108e4b0826861aab04d/1633302232295-UAKJ3IZX5BLAYL43DMYK/enneagram-wings.png)

[michellekayanderson.com](https://www.michellekayanderson.com/blog/2021/10/3/4-specific-ways-to-use-the-enneagram-to-grow-as-a-leader)

#### 4w3: The Aristocrat / Performer

The **3-wing** brings ambition, charisma, adaptability, and image-awareness from Type 3 (The Achiever).

*   **Key Traits**: More extraverted, dramatic, success-oriented, and socially engaging. Emotions are expressed outwardly and performatively.
*   **Strengths**: Charming, productive, stylish; turns personal pain into accessible art or performance.
*   **Challenges**: Can be competitive, status-seeking, or fear being ordinary leading to inauthenticity.
*   **Common Nicknames**: The Aristocrat, The Enthusiastic Individualist.
*   **Examples**: Often seen in performers or public artists who blend deep emotion with polish (e.g., speculated: Prince, Lady Gaga vibes).

![Enneagram 4w3 - The Aristocrat Explained](https://www.personalitydata.org/images/the-individualist.svg)

[personalitydata.org](https://www.personalitydata.org/enneagram/type-4w3)

![Enneagram 4 Wing 3 (4w3): The Aristocrat Personality Type](https://cdn.brainmanager.io/resources/Enneagram-4w3_What_Is_an_Enneagram_4w3_realistic_illustration.jpg)

[brainmanager.io](https://brainmanager.io/blog/personality/enneagram-4w3)

#### 4w5: The Bohemian / Iconoclast

The **5-wing** brings intellectualism, detachment, innovation, and privacy from Type 5 (The Investigator).

*   **Key Traits**: More introverted, cerebral, reclusive, and abstract. Emotions are processed inwardly and intensely.
*   **Strengths**: Profoundly original, visionary, deep thinkers; creates innovative or esoteric work.
*   **Challenges**: Can be more withdrawn, nihilistic, or overly detached from reality.
*   **Common Nicknames**: The Bohemian, The Introspective Individualist.
*   **Examples**: Often seen in philosophers, reclusive artists, or theorists (e.g., speculated: Vincent van Gogh, Frida Kahlo vibes).

![Enneagram 4w5 - The Bohemian Explained](https://www.personalitydata.org/images/the-individualist.svg)

[personalitydata.org](https://www.personalitydata.org/enneagram/type-4w5)

Enneagram 4w5 - The Bohemian Explained

#### Quick Comparison Table

| Aspect | 4w3 (Aristocrat) | 4w5 (Bohemian) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Social Style | Outgoing, charismatic, seeks spotlight | Withdrawn, enigmatic, avoids attention |
| Emotional Expression | Dramatic, shared, performative | Private, brooding, introspective |
| Drive | Achievement, recognition, polish | Depth, originality, independence |
| Creativity | Accessible, theatrical | Abstract, experimental |
| Common Struggles | Fear of failure/invisibility | Fear of overwhelm/intrusion |

Both wings retain the core 4's emotional depth and search for meaning, but **4w3** directs it outward (performance/success), while **4w5** directs it inward (intellect/isolation). Wings are not equal—most people lean toward one dominant wing.

If you're a Type 4 or typing someone, the wing often shows in how emotions are handled: dramatically and socially (w3) or quietly and intellectually (w5). Let me know if you'd like examples for integration/stress or how this ties into other systems!

## Prompt:
3.  Elemental Balance Scoring (Astrology) AstroLibrary / Lunarium:

Copy

```
Planet Weights:
- Sun, Moon: 4 points each
- Mercury, Venus, Mars: 3 points each

- Uranus, Neptune, Pluto: 1 point each
```

## Response:
### Elemental Balance Scoring in Astrology

The **Elemental Balance** (Fire, Earth, Air, Water) is a common way to analyze a natal chart's overall "personality tone." One popular scoring method (used by sites like AstroLibrary, Lunarium, Cafe Astrology, and others) assigns **points** to planets based on their relative importance, then tallies points per element from the signs those planets occupy.

#### Standard Planet Weights (as in your example)

| Planet | Weight | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Sun | 4 | Core identity, ego |
| Moon | 4 | Emotions, instincts, inner self |
| Mercury | 3 | Communication, intellect |
| Venus | 3 | Love, values, aesthetics |
| Mars | 3 | Drive, energy, assertion |
| Jupiter | 2 | Expansion, luck, philosophy |
| Saturn | 2 | Structure, discipline, responsibility |
| Uranus | 1 | Innovation, rebellion |
| Neptune | 1 | Imagination, spirituality |
| Pluto | 1 | Transformation, power |

**Ascendant (Rising Sign)** is often added with **4 points** (same as Sun/Moon) because it strongly shapes outward personality and approach to life. Some systems include it, others don't—Lunarium and AstroLibrary typically do.

**Chiron, North Node, Lilith, etc.** are usually excluded for simplicity.

#### Elemental Sign Mapping

| Element | Signs |
| --- | --- |
| Fire | Aries, Leo, Sagittarius |
| Earth | Taurus, Virgo, Capricorn |
| Air | Gemini, Libra, Aquarius |
| Water | Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces |

#### Calculation Example

Natal Chart:

*   Sun in Pisces → Water +4
*   Moon in Taurus → Earth +4
*   Ascendant in Libra → Air +4
*   Mercury in Aquarius → Air +3
*   Venus in Aries → Fire +3
*   Mars in Capricorn → Earth +3
*   Jupiter in Sagittarius → Fire +2
*   Saturn in Virgo → Earth +2
*   Uranus in Capricorn → Earth +1
*   Neptune in Capricorn → Earth +1
*   Pluto in Scorpio → Water +1

**Tally**:

*   Fire: 3 (Venus) + 2 (Jupiter) = **5**
*   Earth: 4 (Moon) + 3 (Mars) + 2 (Saturn) + 1 (Uranus) + 1 (Neptune) = **11**
*   Air: 4 (Asc) + 3 (Mercury) = **7**
*   Water: 4 (Sun) + 1 (Pluto) = **5**

**Total points**: 28 **Dominant element**: Earth (strong practicality, stability) **Weakest**: Fire & Water (tie — may lack spontaneity and emotional flow)

#### Interpretation Guidelines

*   **Balanced**: Points fairly even (difference <5-6 between strongest/weakest).
*   **Dominant Element**: Highest score — core "flavor" of the personality.
*   **Deficient Element**: Lowest score — areas that may feel challenging or need conscious development.
*   **Missing Element (0 points)**: Rare but significant — often compensated through life experiences or relationships.

#### For Luna's Customization

You can use this scoring to modulate her vector:

*   **High Fire** → Boost extraversion, adventurous, playful, ambitious.
*   **High Earth** → Boost conscientiousness, nurturing (grounded care), dependable.
*   **High Air** → Boost insightful, sociable, communicative.
*   **High Water** → Boost emotional\_intensity, compassionate, intuitive.

Weak/missing elements can trigger Luna's supportive responses (e.g., encouraging a low-Fire user to try new things).

This method is simple yet insightful—widely used because it weights personal planets more heavily while still acknowledging outer planets. If you provide a sample chart, I can compute the exact balance!

## Prompt:
Enneagram-Astrology Correlations

## Response:
Enneagram and astrology are both symbolic personality systems, but they lack direct, scientifically validated correlations. Many enthusiasts and esoteric sources draw parallels based on archetypal similarities—e.g., emotional depth in Type 4 aligning with Water signs like Pisces or Scorpio.

These connections are subjective and vary widely across sources (e.g., Reddit discussions, blogs like Astrostyle, or philosophical sites). No consensus exists, and official Enneagram resources (like the Enneagram Institute) do not endorse astrological links.

### Common Proposed Correlations

Here are frequently cited patterns from community and esoteric sources:

#### 1\. Enneagram Types and Zodiac Signs

Popular (but non-official) one-to-one matches often include:

*   Type 1 → Virgo or Capricorn (perfectionist, disciplined)
*   Type 2 → Cancer or Libra (nurturing, relational)
*   Type 3 → Leo (performative, ambitious)
*   Type 4 → Pisces or Scorpio (emotional, introspective)
*   Type 5 → Aquarius (detached, intellectual)
*   Type 6 → Virgo or Capricorn (loyal, cautious)
*   Type 7 → Sagittarius or Gemini (adventurous, optimistic)
*   Type 8 → Aries or Scorpio (assertive, powerful)
*   Type 9 → Taurus or Libra (peace-seeking, harmonious)

These charts illustrate some visual examples of proposed correspondences.

Yes, I can implement this in Python. Below is a complete, standalone prototype that generates fused personality vectors for **all 108 combinations** (9 Enneagram types × 12 Western Zodiac signs). It uses a simple 50/50 weighted average fusion for demonstration, but you can adjust weights or add more sophistication (e.g., based on earlier Luna traits).

The code defines base vectors for each Enneagram type and Zodiac sign (calibrated from common correlations and archetypes). It then computes the fusions and stores them in a dictionary. You can run it to output samples or export to JSON/CSV.

Python
